Morning Tyler. Thanks for this view. Partnerships is a strong model, and we have multiple examples in other regions where it works for us. Yes we should surely consider looking more into this. That being said I'm sharing below a SS of our commercials for partner model. Also happy to connect you on a call with Shaival who's leading program management from a Partnerships front. ![research-reselling](assets/research/research-reselling/asset-001.jpg)
**Challenges**:
- We need to target long-tail. Either without EOR, or limited coverage in EOR.
- Therefore fewer HCs per client. Still same if not more HCs than a corporate client
- And economics is favorably just 20% lower
If they're a smaller EOR player, looking to service their clients with our global reach, then yes, it's operationally **slighlty cumbersome**. However, that's okay given it's standard in the industry.
If they're (**this is agency model**) looking to **pass on their clinet to us and collect a rev share, then it's not operationally heavy**. They get a bonus for the referal either one time or month on month while we service the client end to end

Gsalary: $12M potential annual volume according to Enterprise. Assume $1000 monthly salary low end worker = 1000 headcounts
  - 新能源行业某龙头企业在筹备海外上市时，就遭遇本地雇佣合规和员工入职难题，内部自建主体、开户和申请税号等流程繁琐耗时。GSalary吉薪的一站式服务则高效解决了这些问题，缩短项目进度，助力企业扫清出海上市障碍
PayinOne (China. CN vs Eng):互联网、智能制造、新能源、储能、光伏、物流、医药、新媒体
  - Payroll volume: $30M a month
  - 10K / 22K HCs
    - $3000 / $1500 payroll per HC per month
  - 1K+ / 3K+ clients
  - 10 / 7 HCs per client

### PEO vs EOR

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331253} -->

Options:
PEO: because employees are their own, can issue ISO, which does not require tax payment for exercising, only for capital gain.
EOR: because employees are not directly employed, they can only receive NSO (non-qualified), which are provided to external advisors etc. Double tax, both income tax and capital gain
**Tax benefits**
- The granting of stock options can have tax benefits to employees. In some cases, such as ISO in the US, the exercising of the option is not subject to income tax. The employee simply pays capital gains tax when any acquired shares are eventually sold. This results in a substantially lessened tax bill. By contrast, with a non-qualified stock option (NSO), tax is due both on the exercising of the option, and the eventual sale of the stock.
From <[https://joinhorizons.com/granting-stock-options-to-foreign-employees/](https://joinhorizons.com/granting-stock-options-to-foreign-employees/)>
Employees consider ISO a better incentive because they are usually**not taxed at the time of exercise**.**However**, if the difference between the exercise price and its current market value is above seventy-three thousand and six hundred dollars ($73,600) for individuals and above one hundred and fourteen thousand six hundred for married persons filing their taxes jointly, they could be tax the[Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT) rate](https://www.irs.gov/taxtopics/tc556)on the ISOs. This is quite unlikely though because, for most US states, AMT is zero. The only states that charge AMT is Colorado, California, Minnesota, and Iowa.**Their the AMT rates are 3.47%, 7%, 5.8%, and 7% respectively**
Additionally, unlike**NSOs that are taxed at the ordinary income tax rate at the time of sale, ISOs are taxed at the capital gains rate which is usually cheaper**. But in order for ISOs to be taxed at the capital gains rate, they have to be held for two (2) years from the grant date and one year after they were exercised.
From <[https://financialfalconet.com/iso-vs-nso-which-is-better/](https://financialfalconet.com/iso-vs-nso-which-is-better/)>

自从我们加入 Customer Service by Amazon (CSBA) 计划以来，我们每周只需回复 5-10 项咨询，工作量减少了约 85%。

**在亚马逊上开店不懂英文可以吗**
1、亚马逊后台页面可以用中文或者英文以及其他语言，这里问题不大。但是亚马逊在listing页面都是本国语言，例如美国就是英语，法国就是法语，等等。所以在写listing的时候，门槛就出现了。
2、个人建议做shopee（虾皮）新手小白在做shopee（虾皮）的时候，建议通过专业的erp来操作，因为erp可以解决翻译，完全不用担心看不到英文，在线编辑，选品，发货，物流，售后等问题，操作简单，提高工作效率。
From <[https://www.amz123.com/ask/hQubGIpE](https://www.amz123.com/ask/hQubGIpE)>
做亚马逊不需要海外客服，如果你做FBA,[亚马逊](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=423277712&content_type=Answer&match_order=2&q=%E4%BA%9A%E9%A9%AC%E9%80%8A&zhida_source=entity)会负责客服发货,亚马逊的客服就是海外客服。假如做亚马逊FBM,其实也没必要海外客服,中国客服都能解决问题,客户是发邮件过来的，只要你在24小时之内回复他就可以。国外客户一般有这个意识，发了邮件之后不会猛催。
链接：https://www.zhihu.com/question/484284292/answer/2162593193

  - Just need an agent

### Warehouse

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331255} -->

根据调研数据，月销50万~100万美元的大卖家，55%有自建或计划自建海外仓；月销100万美元以上的大卖家69%有自建或计划自建海外仓。日均订单量在 1000 单以上时建仓才能平衡仓库、人工等成本。自建仓的卖家中有 81%在美国有仓库，50%在德国有仓库，37%在英国有仓库
？据调研，19%的自建仓大卖家有20个以上的海外仓员工，37%是10~20人，19%是6~10人，25%是6人以下.针对23家服务商仓库的调研发现，服务商平均单仓员工数为12人
受访卖家中，超过 68%的卖家看好抱团建仓。
Assume 10w-50w = 30%, average is 40%
40%*32%=12.8%
From <[https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_logistics/17-88721.html](https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_logistics/17-88721.html)>
50*12*12=7,200
7200*250=1800000
200*5*12=12,000

### ARPU

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E185059447023408357961853258456561674985410} -->

大多数 PEO 公司都会对自己提供的服务收取管理费，管理费的定价方式通常有以下两种（可任选其一）：每名员工每月收取固定费用，通常为每位员工每年 1,000 美元左右；或者按薪资总额的一定比例收取，通常为 2-11%。有些平台可能还会对自己的人力资源功能、报告或某些集成功能收取使用费。
欢迎向 Deel 垂询定制报价，了解如何利用我们的 HR 套件（员工人数少于 200 人的企业可免费使用）和经济实惠的企业级 HR 福利来降低管理成本。
From <[https://www.deel.com/zh-cn/peo/](https://www.deel.com/zh-cn/peo/)>

**4. Chiplego**
Chiplego is a Chinese company specializing in semiconductor technology and integrated circuit design. They focus on developing advanced chip solutions for consumer electronics, telecommunications, and automotive applications. Their products are known for high performance and energy efficiency, catering to both domestic and international markets.
**5. Dghire**
Dghire is a Chinese human resources and recruitment firm offering talent acquisition, staffing solutions, and HR consulting services. They assist businesses in various sectors to identify and recruit qualified professionals, providing services such as executive search, temporary staffing, and workforce management solutions.
**6. Neusoft**
Neusoft Corporation is a leading IT solutions and services provider in China, established in 1991. They offer a wide range of services, including software development, IT consulting, and system integration. Neusoft serves industries such as healthcare, finance, automotive, and education, with a focus on innovation and digital transformation.
**7. Imperial Tours**
Imperial Tours is a luxury travel company specializing in high-end tours and personalized travel experiences in China. They cater to discerning travelers seeking bespoke itineraries, cultural immersion, and exclusive access to unique destinations and events across China.

## PayPal China strategy

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331190} -->

1. PayPal Strategy:
  1. Still focus on DTC and SMB B2B:
    1. DTC - now directly embeds in website beside Shopify/ Shopline integrations
  1. 合法合规安全：收款宝需要更严格的审核、需要提供货物贸易凭据
  1. Maximize自己钱包里的余额提现的生意
    1. Cheaper, especially if <$10K. Cheaper than traditional bank transfer, and PSP (because charges $35+10 to withdraw to PSP)
    1. No limit:因为有货物贸易凭证，并且直接人民币到账，不占用5W额度
    1. Faster
  1. Monetization:
    1. Only cheaper for smaller orders because one-time fee is waived ($35)
    1. Monetize 0.5% fee (instead of 0) and fx (sometimes better, sometimes worse than banks)
    1. Larger amount may still use收款宝to avoid 5W quota
  1. Use case:
    1. Still use PSP such as Payo if amount is larger. (0.5% adds up to outsize $35+10 PSP withdrawal fee)
1. Main use case for China vendors:
  1. Check out / B2B快闪: 3 modules in one (QR code, link, html API).集成Shoplazza, Shopyy, Ueeshop, BigCommerce和WooCommerce
  1. Check out全球收单：allows Apple Pay, Google Pay, Credit Card, + PayPal
  1. W2B: to China (expensive, uses quota), to HK
  1. PON China:  Uses foreign currencies in PayPal to pay CNY (and they don't need PayPal account)
1. China roadmap
  1. 2023.5:跨境收款宝,为中国商户量身定制，无需跳转第三方，从点击“确认收款”到人民币入账中国境内银行账户
    1. Before that, $35 per transaction, 1-7 days
    1. After that, stricter KYC, need receipts, but lower fee, 4 hours,不占用5万美金额度
    1. Both charges hefty fx fee
  1. 2024.9:全球支付平台:DTC,粉丝群, WhatsApp/展会
**收取场景**
将您在PayPal账户内持有的资金以人民币结算至您在PayPal中国账户所关联的国内银行账户。
![research-paypal-china-strategy](assets/research/research-paypal-china-strategy/asset-001.png)
From <[https://www.paypal.cn/portal/fees](https://www.paypal.cn/portal/fees)>
[image: {A86A36C0-A2F1-0BE4-2DD4-B8CFCF77A3B9}{93}{B0}]
- 可提现的金额为过去180天的货物贸易收款金额。额度每天都会自动跳转。非货物贸易收款不支持跨境收款宝提现。例如联盟佣金，或者朋友之间的转账，是不支持跨境收款宝提现的。
From <[https://www.waimaob2c.com/paypal-tixian](https://www.waimaob2c.com/paypal-tixian)>

### Paypal payout process

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331191} -->

2、PayPal如何转账给银行？
可以将PayPal资金转账本地银行账户、香港银行账户以及美国银行账户：
- 前往账户首页。
- 在PayPal余额下点击提现。
- 选择提现方式，然后按照说明完成转账提现。
1. 转账到中国银行的账户
手续费为转账金额的1.2%，收款币种是CNY，2-3个工作日到账。
2. 通过电汇转账到中国的银行账户
1) 每笔转账手续费是35美金，认证账户没有提现限额。收款币种是USD，3-7个工作日到账。
2) 转账失败的退还费用是15美金，用户将自行承担退还费用以及可能产生的汇兑损失。
3. 转账到香港的银行账户
1) 3–6个工作日;
2) 收款币种为HKD;
3) 低于1000港元的提现，每笔提现3.5港元，1000港币及以上的免收手续费;
4) 转账失败的退还费用是20港币， 用户将自行承担退还费用以及产生的汇兑损失。
4. 转账到美国的银行账户
免手续费，3-4个工作日到账，收款币种为HKD。
3、如何将PayPal的钱转到支付宝？
paypal不能提现到支付宝，他们是不同的第三方支付系统，相互之间不能转账。paypal只能提现到绑定paypal的银行卡中，这张银行卡必须是多币种储蓄账户，因为提现的币种是美元。
具体提现步骤如下：
1. 登入到PayPal。
1. 进入“我的PayPal”。
1. 点击“提款”。
1. 然后点击“其它选项”。
1. 会看到有4种提现方式供选择，选择收取款项的方式。比如，选择中国工商银行（多币种储蓄账户）。提现完成。需要3-7个工作日到账。
PayPal单笔提现的最小额度为150美元，最大额度为100000美元，手续费为每笔35美元。到帐时间： 3-7个工作日；提现失败的退还费用是15美金, 用户将自行承担退还费用以及可能产生的汇兑损失。
4、亲友转账
用Paypal转账是很多人会选择给亲友支付的一种方式，然而！使用Paypal 的 Friends and Family转账方式转账时，一定要注意！因为FF这种我们大家常用的亲友间的转账方式，是不在Dispute的范围内的！也就是说，一旦转账的过程中出现任何问题，Paypal是不负责任的，包括转账转错人等等！
所以在大家使用Paypal转账功能给亲朋好友时，一定要注意再三确认好对方的账户信息！以免造成一些麻烦！！
如何从PayPal提现？
你可以将PayPal账户中的余额提现到你在中国大陆、中国香港或美国开设的银行账户。为完成此操作，请关联你的银行账户至PayPal，并确保你的用户名与银行账户开户名一致。除提现至银行账户外，你还可以申请支票。
在将资金提现至你的银行账户之前，还需要验证你的账户。
在计算机上从PayPal账户中提现的方法如下：
1. 前往账户首页。
1. 在页面左侧点击“PayPal余额”下方的转账。
1. 选择提现方式和要提现的余额。
1. 按照说明完成提现。
不同PayPal提现方式，处理时间和最低金额可能会有所不同，具体取决于提现类型：
![research-paypal-payout-process](assets/research/research-paypal-payout-process/asset-001.jpg)
Paypal手续费
用户使用paypal付款是没有任何额外的手续费，使用PayPal支付，无论是通过信用卡、借记卡或PayPal余额付款，买家均不必支付任何手续费的。手续费通常是由卖家承担的。PayPal手续费一共分为三种：交易手续费、提现手续费和货币兑换手续费。
1. PayPal交易/转账手续费
A帐户转给B帐户100美金，则paypal向B帐户收取交易手续费。只有在成功完成交易时才需支付交易费。此外，满足一定条件后，用户的月销售额越高，所需支付的费率就越低。
费用计算：交易手续费目前常见的有两种收取方式，分别为普通费率和小额商品费率。
（1）普通费率
亚太地区统一收费标准，按阶梯分段收取，交易收款总额越大，费率越低。
新账号是4.4%+0.3,当月收款大于三千美金后可以申请卖家优惠（只需要申请一次，以后会自动调整）
当优惠费率申请成功后，费率变动如下：
- 当月累积收款3000usd以下是：4.4%+0.3，
- 当月累积收款3000-10000usd是：3.9%+0.3，
- 当月累积收款10000-100000usd是：3.7%+0.3，
- 当月累积收款100000usd以上是：3.4%+0.3。
- 不申请永远是4.4%+0.3。
（2）小额商品费率
小额商品费率需特别申请，申请后将不能再使用默认的普通费率。
- 收费计算：6%+0.05（固定收费）
备注：PayPal交易手续费月收款超过3000美金可以申请下调
2、PayPal提现手续费
提现方式:提现至美国银行帐户、电汇至中国银行帐户、提现至香港银行帐户、向paypal申请支票。
PayPal提现手续费计算
- 提现至美国银行帐户:免费
- 提现至香港银行帐户:1,000港币以下，手续费3.5港币；1,000港币以上，免费
- 电汇到中国银行帐户:每次提现收取35美金，不限金额
- 向paypal申请支票:每次提现收取5美金
From <[https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/661423936](https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/661423936)>

### Why use Payoneer to w2b

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331192} -->

目前中国大陆地区的PayPal提现有4种方法：
1、（不推荐）直接电汇至中国大陆地区的银行账户，每笔手续费$35，占用中国公民每年5万美元的[外汇额度](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=238716656&content_type=Article&match_order=1&q=%E5%A4%96%E6%B1%87%E9%A2%9D%E5%BA%A6&zhida_source=entity)。但大部分银行会直接拒绝来自PayPal的汇款，就算有少数银行接受了，也会设定各种限制条件，要求你提供交易记录、合同、发货单等各种证明材料，否则资金就原路退回（手续费不退），耗时久，成功率低，很不友好。
2、（不推荐）提现至中国香港的银行账户，1千港币以上免手续费，但是每笔有2.5%的[货币转换](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=238716656&content_type=Article&match_order=1&q=%E8%B4%A7%E5%B8%81%E8%BD%AC%E6%8D%A2&zhida_source=entity)费，因为账户中是美元，但提现入账到香港的银行账户却变成了港币，中间要扣2.5%，真离谱。
3、（不推荐）通过支票提现，每笔手续费$5，从国外邮寄支票到国内，要等好几个月，还容易丢，等收到支票去[银行结汇](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=238716656&content_type=Article&match_order=1&q=%E9%93%B6%E8%A1%8C%E7%BB%93%E6%B1%87&zhida_source=entity)同样会碰到要你提供各种证明材料。
4、（推荐）提现至美国的银行账户，每笔手续费$35，我们需要先提现到第三方收款工具Payoneer（[派安盈](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=238716656&content_type=Article&match_order=1&q=%E6%B4%BE%E5%AE%89%E7%9B%88&zhida_source=entity)）的美国账户，然后直接提现到国内银行卡，按照[中国银行](https://zhida.zhihu.com/search?content_id=238716656&content_type=Article&match_order=1&q=%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E9%93%B6%E8%A1%8C&zhida_source=entity)实时外汇买入价算，正常1小时内就能到账，手续费1.2%~0.5%，不占用每年5万美元的外汇额度。
From <[https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/677639360](https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/677639360)>

From <[https://ir.aboutamazon.com/news-release/news-release-details/2025/Amazon-com-Announces-First-Quarter-Results/](https://ir.aboutamazon.com/news-release/news-release-details/2025/Amazon-com-Announces-First-Quarter-Results/)>
UBS noted Amazon’s second-quarter guidance implies roughly5% growth in gross merchandise value (GMV), excluding foreign exchange, a slowdown that likely reflects consumer price sensitivity following tariff shifts.
From <[https://www.proactiveinvestors.co.uk/companies/news/1070629/amazon-well-positioned-for-aws-acceleration-trade-tailwinds-despite-macro-risks-analysts-1070629.html](https://www.proactiveinvestors.co.uk/companies/news/1070629/amazon-well-positioned-for-aws-acceleration-trade-tailwinds-despite-macro-risks-analysts-1070629.html)>
See also: Amazon says it won't show how tariffs are impacting prices. Why that's a good sign.
One thing that's not so much of a worry for Amazon investors is the end of the "de minimis" exemption, which let businesses import parcels worth under $800 into the U.S. without facing tariffs.
"While there may be a significant number of sellers shipping goods just under the $800 limit, we believe that they ultimately accounted fora small or mid-single-digit percentof GMV," or gross merchandise volume, Ju wrote.
In fact, Amazon could end up benefiting as Temu and Shein, Chinese competitors that more heavily relied on the "de minimis" exemption, have to raise prices or scale back their push into the U.S.
"Although Amazon Haul will also feel the impact of nullifying the 'de minimis' exemption, we believe the net result will bepositive for Amazon and its peers," Monness, Crespi, Hardt & Co. analyst Brian White wrote.
The bigger problem for Amazon could be around general purchasing, with the UBS team estimating that at least half of what's sold on Amazon could face price adjustments due to tariff costs. "Consumers therefore might have to make more difficult choices on where to allocate their dollars," Ju wrote.
The good news, analysts say, is that Amazon might emerge from this period stronger. "Retail revenue growth and margins both contracted five years ago during the last China tariff hike, and again in the inflation-led slowdown in 2022," Bernstein analyst Mark Shmulik wrote. "In both of these instances, Amazon's [e-commerce] market share and retail marginsnot only recovered, but improvedpost-macro shock."
The bad news is that investors might need to reset their growth expectations for the year. UBS's Ju now expects8% growth in U.S. volumes this year, versus his prior estimate of 10.4%.
From <[https://www.morningstar.com/news/marketwatch/20250430149/amazon-could-emerge-stronger-from-tariffs-but-earnings-will-show-how-much-pain-that-requires](https://www.morningstar.com/news/marketwatch/20250430149/amazon-could-emerge-stronger-from-tariffs-but-earnings-will-show-how-much-pain-that-requires)>
North America segment sales increased8% year-over-year, international segment sales increased 5%
From <[https://www.ecommercebytes.com/2025/05/01/amazon-grows-north-america-sales-8-in-first-quarter/](https://www.ecommercebytes.com/2025/05/01/amazon-grows-north-america-sales-8-in-first-quarter/)>
To some extent, we've seen some heightened buying in certain categories that may indicate stocking up in advance of any potential tariff impact. We also have not seen the average selling price of retail items appreciably go up yet. Some of this reflects some forward buying we did in our first-party selling and some of that reflects some advanced inbounding our third-party sellers have done, but a fair amount of this is that most sellers just haven't changed pricing yet.
From <[https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript)>
As it relates to China, retailers who aren't buying directly from China are typically buying from companies who themselves are buying from China, marking these items up, rebranding and selling to U.S. Consumers. These retailers are buying the product at a higher price than Chinese sellers selling directly to U.S. consumers in our marketplace. So, the total tariff will be higher for these retailers than for China Direct Sellers.
From <[https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript)>
In the first quarter, our Everyday Essentials grew more than twice as fast as the rest of our business and represented one out of every three units sold in the U.S. and Amazon.
From <[https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4780834-amazon-com-inc-amzn-q1-2025-earnings-call-transcript)>

“At some point, I’m going to lower them, because otherwise, you could never do business with them, and they want to do business very much,” Trump said on NBC’s Meet the Press.

61%的卖家计划将在欧洲市场寻求新的增长高地。
此外，鉴于欧洲市场的流量相对较小，他建议卖家不要懒惰，应多开设站点如（英国、德国、法国、意大利、西班牙等），并利用泛欧计划，把货寄到德国来稳定订单量。
不过也有卖家表示“欧洲税也高啊，vat各种合规和服务费加上平台佣金尾程派送费用，这些算下来都快70%了，随便做点广告促销，利润非常薄了，同样价格卖美国起码能多赚30%”
From <[https://www.cifnews.com/article/173308](https://www.cifnews.com/article/173308)>
分析公司Vizion数据显示，运往美国的集装箱订单近一周内从51.6万TEU（标准箱）降至16.9万TEU，跌幅高达67%。亚洲至美西航线货运量更是从14.8万TEU骤降至5.4万TEU，几乎腰斩。此外，宁波航运交易所的运价指数持续探底，美西航线运价单周下跌2.3%，美东航线下跌0.5%。船公司为稳定运价，紧急宣布削减30%跨太平洋航线运力。
From <[https://www.cifnews.com/article/173308](https://www.cifnews.com/article/173308)>
雨果跨境对卖家进行的一项调研结果显示，为应对关税冲击，16.86%的卖家选择不涨价，65.91%的卖家涨价幅度为10%-30%，10.19%的卖家涨价幅度为30%-50%，1.76%的卖家涨价幅度为50%-70%，0.75%的卖家涨价幅度为70%-100%，1.89%的卖家涨价幅度超过100%。
From <[https://www.cifnews.com/article/173131](https://www.cifnews.com/article/173131)>
“我们跨境电商自主销售这一块目前没什么影响，因为价格我们是可控的。”张晨告诉盐财经。更重要的是，因为提前听到关税可能上涨的风声，上个月，张晨就已经大量向美出口备货，“目前的（海外仓）库存还足以支撑两三个月的销售”，对利润影响不大。
她盼着再过一两个月，根据新的需求回调产量，维持工厂运转。这样的生产调整，是她做外贸的常态。张晨担心的，只是涨价之后，客户购买欲望会下降；同时，她希望同行不要内卷打价格战，挤压本就微薄的利润。
From <[https://finance.sina.com.cn/tech/roll/2025-04-12/doc-inesxaat7220677.shtml](https://finance.sina.com.cn/tech/roll/2025-04-12/doc-inesxaat7220677.shtml)>
茅鑫销售的鞋服平均客单价为30美元，涨价并不明显到足以让美国消费者敏感。因此，他并不认为加高关税会很快侵蚀他们的利润。尤其在复杂的海关系统里，对于货物价值的界定一直是一个难题，也存在低于货值进行申报的空间，行业内低申报行为很普遍，高额关税能否实际到位执行等都是问题。

![research-apr-customer-research](assets/research/research-apr-customer-research/asset-002.png)
[https://kjgcl.com/%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%8D%96%E5%AE%B6%E4%B8%8D%E5%BF%8D%E4%BA%86%EF%BC%81%E5%AE%89%E5%85%8B%E5%85%88%E6%B6%A8%E4%BB%B718/](https://kjgcl.com/%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%8D%96%E5%AE%B6%E4%B8%8D%E5%BF%8D%E4%BA%86%EF%BC%81%E5%AE%89%E5%85%8B%E5%85%88%E6%B6%A8%E4%BB%B718/)
跨境电商卖家开始核算新的成本，并试探性提价。随着包税运费不断提高，一向催着客户发货的货代公司，这几天也开始劝客户“理性发货”。
产品货值较低的外贸商，在与美国客户的交易中溢价能力较弱，易受关税冲击影响。不过，或许是由于部分美国批发客户暂缓了订单，消费者转到线上去购买，反而让一些跨境电商卖家的订单有所增加。
亚马逊卖家在美国的正常库存通常是2个月的销量，目前卖的货是2个月前发出的，并未包含关税，因此在美国消费者端的涨价还不太明显。
但是，随着关税影响落地，4月10日之后发货的商品，都需要重新核算成本，根据新的成本调整定价策略，再以新的订单量来确定合理库存。
“涨价是肯定的，问题是涨多少。这需要时间来测试。”王敏说。消费端对于产品涨价的接受程度至关重要，目前他对一款产品尝试性涨价了6%，订单量暂未受到影响，但不知道未来会如何。涨价除了估计消费端的接受度，测算自己的成本之外，还要观察其他卖家的跟进程度。
From <[https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html](https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html)>
“近两个月是重要的价格测试窗口期，因为海外仓内还有一批存货，成本比较低。如果价格上涨导致订单下滑得太快，算下来整体亏本的话，就不能做了。”王敏说。
由于不少外贸企业是委托货代公司办理双清保税，因此关税上升体现在运费里。
证券时报记者从业内了解到，多家国际货代公司已于4月10日下午向外贸企业发出上调运费的通知，因受美国所谓“对等关税”的影响，对所有美线海运非自税渠道（即保税）的客户，每公斤运费加价幅度在3.5元左右。4月2日以来，美国对中国进口商品征收所谓“对等关税”，不同货代公司的美线海运保税价格从每公斤4元至6元，上涨到9至15元，上涨幅度超100%。自税（自主报税）的企业不受影响，关税由企业另行承担。
From <[https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html](https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html)>
招商基金研究部首席经济学家李湛建议，针对受美国加税冲击严重的机电、纺织、跨境电商等行业，建议设立6000亿元外贸企业纾困基金，提供1年期贴息贷款及汇率避险工具。对于跨境电商企业，可试点海外仓直邮包裹增值税即征即退政策，降低物流成本15%左右。
From <[https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html](https://www.stcn.com/article/detail/1657618.html)>
销售书包和蓝牙音响的卖家Dave Fong表示，他已在美国市场将部分商品价格提高了三成。同时，他也在减少库存，并大幅削减在亚马逊平台上的广告投入——过去这些广告支出曾占他总收入的40%。
“我们已无法再依赖美国市场，”Fong表示。“我们必须减少在美投资，将更多资源分配到欧洲、加拿大、墨西哥和其他地区。”
在深圳从事亚马逊销售已有七年的Brian Miller则表示，目前他没有动力推出任何新产品。他预计，等现有库存在一到两个月后售罄后，他和其他卖家将不得不大幅涨价。
他举例称，自家售价20美元、生产成本3美元的儿童积木玩具，在新关税下制造成本将上升至7美元。为了维持利润率，售价至少得涨价20%，而价格较高的玩具涨幅可能高达50%。
From <[https://www.dw.com/zh/%E5%8F%97%E7%89%B9%E6%9C%97%E6%99%AE%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E%E5%86%B2%E5%87%BB-%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E4%BA%9A%E9%A9%AC%E9%80%8A%E5%8D%96%E5%AE%B6%E8%A2%AB%E8%BF%AB%E6%B6%A8%E4%BB%B7%E6%88%96%E6%92%A4%E7%A6%BB/a-72208169](https://www.dw.com/zh/%E5%8F%97%E7%89%B9%E6%9C%97%E6%99%AE%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E%E5%86%B2%E5%87%BB-%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E4%BA%9A%E9%A9%AC%E9%80%8A%E5%8D%96%E5%AE%B6%E8%A2%AB%E8%BF%AB%E6%B6%A8%E4%BB%B7%E6%88%96%E6%92%A4%E7%A6%BB/a-72208169)>

Kingsway Chen:
1) Facing tariff, B2C sellers are: a) waiting to see the market/competitor changes; b) preparing more storage in oversea warehouse before May 2nd. Major logistic companies (express and small package service) QoQ daily US vols increased by 10%-30%. Take UPS as example, daily US vols increase from 250T to 300T+. c) based on tariff, seller might raise their selling rate, but they’re very cautious on this action due to potential traffic decline.
2) If T86 cancelled by May 2nd, CN sellers are looking for the transit export or special declaration channel to minimize the tariff impact in US.
3) Sellers are actively developing new marketplaces in Europe, Canada, Mexica, South American, Mid-East and Russia.
4) NA contributes about 60% total GMV of major Amazon sellers. Considered the uncertain Tariff situation, we're looking for the more support in EU, to maintain a sustainable growth in EU and other countries out of US.
5) Action of competitors: Develop relative events and salons for business out of US.
Jason Liu:
卫哲认为，未来两到三个月内，中美之间一定会有一个解决方案，因为7-8月ODM企业必须确定美国圣诞节订单。若彼时局势仍陷泥潭，“美国将会迎来一个空空荡荡的圣诞节”。
不过，也有受访者提到，目前超九成运送至美国的商品是通过“灰清”的方式入关。所谓灰清，即出口商将货物交由专门的清关公司处理，用极低的成本过关。上述从业者认为，关税政策的变化主要影响的是清关效率。
至于关税到底会否直接消除小包生意？“风浪越大鱼越贵，总有牛人能灰清过去。”
海外仓会否成为跨境卖家的标配？当然
一位曾经的Temu商家说，Temu已经明显偏向半托管模式（即配置海外仓），“搜索页第一页靠前的基本是半托，带Local标签的流量会好”。
东南亚＞墨西哥。
他的观察是，墨西哥政府正通过削减关税优惠、提高进口免交增值税门槛等方式大力促进本地化供应链的发展。“这一点类似中国从以加工贸易为主转变为发展本地产业链。”
这也要求中国出海企业应该提早布局，对墨西哥的供应链转型本地化。“我们会要求当地按照实际原产地来申报以及出具原产地证，不得造假。”
TikTok Shop近日向美区卖家发布通知，将自5月2日起执行新规：对入境商品征收30%从价税，并分阶段加收单件25美元（6月1日前）或50美元（6月1日后）的特定关税，同时要求承运人缴纳国际保证金。而Temu和Shein目前尚公布应对举措。
就在关税政策沸沸扬扬的这几天， Temu 与DHL集团签署了备忘录。备忘录称，DHL集团将运用其专业物流能力支持Temu在欧洲的本地化运营，特别是其“本地对本地”商业模式（local-to-local）。
欧洲是第一选择。亚马逊FBA（物流服务）比第三方价格还低。但在欧洲，亚马逊就没有类似的地位，比如FBA的费用要6-7英镑，较第三方要贵出3-4英镑。
事实上，Temu也的确在向欧洲挺进。一位跨境电商部门运营总监说，他在前几天刚接到Temu招商人员的电话，对方称接下来“将发力欧洲市场”。
也有受访者的答案是日本
比起数倍、数十倍成本完全到美国建设，在墨西哥“曲线救国”仍然算得上制造业应对复杂中美局势的最优解之一。甚至，有些企业家要求要完全（至少表面上）成为墨西哥公司。前述总经理提到一个“垒防火墙到极端的案例”——有一家中国企业，但要求挂新加坡国旗。“名义上的总经理”是美国人。
对于想要打入、发展美国市场的公司而言，取道拉丁美洲仍是优先级最高的策略。其合理考虑顺序为：美国-墨西哥-哥伦比亚/巴西。
From[暗涌Waves](https://mp.weixin.qq.com/s/tGQxP5TvbJx2iO-VMJPHQw)[https://mp.weixin.qq.com/s/tGQxP5TvbJx2iO-VMJPHQw](https://mp.weixin.qq.com/s/tGQxP5TvbJx2iO-VMJPHQw)
张晨是珠三角一电单车工厂老板娘。这3天以来，她已经记不清出口美国的关税加了多少轮。她必须拿笔记下来，才能弄明白不断变化的关税数字，将怎样影响她的生意和利润。
张晨家入门级产品的客单价约为300美元，现有生产部分的毛利率虽仅有13%，但面向欧美销售，基本上是按出厂价翻数倍销售，足以对冲关税。但为保持利润，张晨做好了涨价的准备。
“我们跨境电商自主销售这一块目前没什么影响，因为价格我们是可控的。”张晨告诉盐财经。更重要的是，因为提前听到关税可能上涨的风声，上个月，张晨就已经大量向美出口备货，“目前的（海外仓）库存还足以支撑两三个月的销售”，对利润影响不大。
“美国客户定制的那部分订单，我们也不敢出太多货。这周我们也就只出了一条柜子（注：运输集装箱货柜），平时的话每周都有出个三条。”张晨趋于保守，观望形势。
From <[https://finance.sina.com.cn/tech/roll/2025-04-12/doc-inesxaat7220677.shtml](https://finance.sina.com.cn/tech/roll/2025-04-12/doc-inesxaat7220677.shtml)>
拿我们出售的台灯为例，原本它的售价是274.99美元，但现在我们需要为其支付的关税就超过了100美元。
我确实考虑过在美国生产我们的商品。但我发现在美国，没有任何一家工厂能够像中国的工厂一样实现我们的要求，更何况在中国有我们所需的所有配件。

### Global reciprocal tariff

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331207} -->

Answer:
  1. Syndicated effort across small package and trade corridors
  1. Does not favor oversea factory cost (Vietnam / Thai needs to have 40% tariff saving to make sense)
  1. Only corridors left are LATAM and Africa, but sellers will not rush, and even higher risk
  1. Price increase will certainly reduce demands from USA. Even products not directly from China, like iPhone, will increase prices
  1. Certainly will shift to other marketplaces
  1. Some will leverage their US entity, to explore local manufacturing
  1. High margin sellers least impacted: electronics. Fashion, furniture hit worldwide
  1. Will hurt Vietnam and Thai manufacturing upgrade… and even harder to move supply chain out of China
**What's happening**:
  1. Syndicated pressure on China sellers:
    1. Tariff increase on China totaled 54% this year (effective April 9)
    1. Simultaneous shutdown of  "work-arounds": small packages tariff (resume May 2), trade corridors (reciprocal tariff on April 9)
  1. Chinese sellers may maintain market share (most of sellers' supply chain will be hit, unless 100% U.S. content)
  1. Regardless, inflation expected will hurt overall consumer demands in U.S., and reduce eComm volume
**Changes in our takeaways**:
  1. Relocating supply chain may not be effective, and certainly more complex now
  1. Current tariff is stretching the limit of seller's resilience. Shifting towards RoW marketplaces will accelerate
  1. We need to prepare for material revenue impact if the current tariff regime persist
**How China sellers may react**:
  1. Increase price to partially offset tariff cost
  1. Prepare to expand in ROW eComm platforms
  1. Mitigation options are limited as Southeast Asia faces high tariff. LATAM faces low tariff (10%), but sellers are cautious
  1. Increase U.S. footprint: those with a U.S. entity may consider move part of the supply chain to U.S. Those without a U.S. entity may consider setting it up to lower cost especially for DTC webstore
  1. Drop shippers, webstore sellers reliant on small package will have to reconfigure their supply chain
**Actions PayO can take**:
  1. Support sellers and provide resources as they expand to RoW marketplaces (marketplace relation, PS, Green Channel product)
  1. Support sellers' U.S. entities, e.g. check-out solution for U.S. entities
  1. Smooth pay-out as customers face increasing financial strain (increased logistics cost + tariff)
  1. New sellers, SMBs, Drop shippers, Low-margin category sellers are hit disproportionately. We need to think if adjustment in resource / KPIs is needed. We expect fluctuations / concerns from these group of customers
  1. Faster growth in Rest-of-World marketplaces: Enterprise
  1. Support customers with U.S. entities
  - (not included in the graph: China faces 54% tariff increase in total. Canada and Mexico still face 25% tariff)

  1. Impasse, inflation, US walks down

#### US motivation

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331208} -->

1. US is the world's buyer, so other countries cannot just do business among themselves
1. US needs to be able to have resilience in semiconductor, industrial
1. Build automated factory to replace China's Foxconn, although takes time, but meanwhile will provide employment to US workers (when responding to how it hurts farmers)
1. Ultimate goal: move factories to US to have resilience, to fix deficit - zero:zero will not fix deficit b/c US is the buyer!
1. Why EU has a surplus with us? They are not special in any way
1. Us is 17% owned by the world now, and increasingly

First Sale for Export:
  - Could be valued based on intermediary transaction, either related party or 3rd party. Could be a wholesaler or intermediary (in the 3rd party case, use intermediary's invoice)
  - **Arm’s-Length Pricing**: Transactions must be independent and commercially reasonable, ensuring there is no undue influence on pricing due to relationships between the parties.
  - **Clear Export Designation**: At the time of sale, goods must be explicitly committed for export to the United States, with documentation clearly proving their intended destination.
  - From <[https://www.welcometodojo.com/insights/what-is-first-sale-and-how-can-it-reduce-your-tariff-impact](https://www.welcometodojo.com/insights/what-is-first-sale-and-how-can-it-reduce-your-tariff-impact)>

### April update (policy)

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331211} -->

国务院关税税则委员会的公告指，自2025年4月10日12时01分起，对原产于美国的进口商品加征关税。有关事项如下：一、对原产于美国的所有进口商品，在现行适用关税税率基础上加征34%关税；二、现行保税、减免税政策不变，此次加征的关税不予减免；三、2025年4月10日12时01分之前，货物已从启运地启运，并于2025年4月10日12时01分至2025年5月13日24时进口的，不加征本公告规定加征的关税。
此外，中国商务部宣布将16家美国实体列入出口管制管控名单，禁止对其出口两用物项。相关实体包括：高点航空技术公司、环球世界物流公司、Source Intelligence公司、美国繁荣联盟、内华达山脉公司等。中国商务部还发布不可靠实体清单工作机制关于将斯凯迪奥公司等11家美国企业列入不可靠实体清单的公告，禁止相关企业从事与中国有关的进出口活动，及在中国境内新增投资。
与此同时，中国商务部会同海关总署4月4日发布关于对钐、钆、铽、镝、镥、钪、钇等7类中重稀土相关物项实施出口管制措施的公告，并于发布之日起正式实施。同日，中国商务部发布公告，公布对原产于美国、印度的进口相关医用CT球管发起反倾销立案调查。
Publicité
海关总署则发布第54号和第55号公告，称由于相关进口产品存在检验检疫问题，决定暂停1家美国涉事企业高粱输华资质，3家美国涉事企业禽肉骨粉输华资质，并暂停2家美国涉事企业禽肉产品输华。
同样在当天，市场监管总局宣布，因杜邦中国集团有限公司涉嫌违反《中华人民共和国反垄断法》，市场监管总局对该公司开展立案调查。杜邦中国是美国化工业巨头杜邦公司的子公司。
From <[https://www.rfi.fr/cn/%E7%BB%8F%E8%B4%B8/20250404-%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%A3%E5%B8%83%E5%8F%8D%E5%88%B6%E5%AF%B9%E7%AD%89%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E%E7%B3%BB%E5%88%97%E6%8E%AA%E6%96%BD-%E5%8C%85%E6%8B%AC%E5%AF%B9%E7%BE%8E%E5%9B%BD%E8%BE%93%E5%8D%8E%E5%95%86%E5%93%81%E5%8A%A0%E5%BE%8134-%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E](https://www.rfi.fr/cn/%E7%BB%8F%E8%B4%B8/20250404-%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%A3%E5%B8%83%E5%8F%8D%E5%88%B6%E5%AF%B9%E7%AD%89%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E%E7%B3%BB%E5%88%97%E6%8E%AA%E6%96%BD-%E5%8C%85%E6%8B%AC%E5%AF%B9%E7%BE%8E%E5%9B%BD%E8%BE%93%E5%8D%8E%E5%95%86%E5%93%81%E5%8A%A0%E5%BE%8134-%E5%85%B3%E7%A8%8E)>

Lutnick went on say the steeper reciprocal tariffs were still on track.

WASHINGTON, March 26 (Reuters) - U.S. President[Donald Trump](https://www.reuters.com/world/us/donald-trump/)said on Wednesday he would be willing to reduce tariffs on China to get a deal done with TikTok's Chinese parent ByteDance to sell the short video app used by 170 million Americans.
From <[https://www.reuters.com/technology/trump-says-he-may-give-china-reduction-tariffs-get-tiktok-deal-done-2025-03-26/](https://www.reuters.com/technology/trump-says-he-may-give-china-reduction-tariffs-get-tiktok-deal-done-2025-03-26/)>
**Mar**
- **[Market trend]**
  - **Merchants' Coping Strategies in Response to US Tax Policy Changes**:  customer #Zhenxian is selling solar panels. Before, in order to avoid the US tax increase policy on the solar industry, the customer transferred theproduction line to Vietnam. However, because of concerns that the new tariff policy will verify the nationality of the owner of the goods, the customer has not fully started the new production line and is still waiting to see. (Chris Zhang)
  - **Concerns from Big Distribution Seller**: Big distribution seller #Hengxinghong provided feedback that they’re controlling the business volume from US, hoping to control the portion to under 60%, increasing investment on other markets like EU at the same time. Because they’re concerned about US policy especially on customs and tax. Also, some main marketplaces like Walmart and Temu now restrict the SKU or store quantity, that will also cause obstacles to their distribution model. (Frank Lin)
  - **Control non complianct goods within tariff exemption limit**: (by Daisy)
    - On March 17th, US Customs CSMS (Cargo System Information Service) released a notice regarding a "Business Environment (ACE) Development and Deployment Plan", which shows a deployment date of September 2025 to be able to strictly control non compliant goods within the exmption limit.
    - Many sellers believe that this announcement means that the $800 exemption policy is expected to be extended until September. However, some sellers have also stated that the CBP announcement focuses on system deployment updates and does not explicitly state whether it is related to the cancellation of the $800 tariff exemption. The policy of the $800 exemption may be cancelled at any time.
- **[Market trend]**
  - WalMart requested Chinese suppliers to significantlyreduce prices: this is mainly to shift the cost from US tariffs ~20% to Chinese suppliers. On March 11,  China Ministry of Commerce talked with WalMart regarding that.  1.this may cause the decline on goods quality. 2. this may impact the standard of international trading. 3. The newly effected 20% tariffs by the United States harms both Chinese and American companies, and should be taking by both parties. (by Daisy)
  - Merchants’ responding to US market risk:
    - #Ipegtop (home), who’s ahybrid sellerin US market, shared that they will cut investment on marketplace like Amazon and forecast a–50% YoY changein 2025. Because their boss assumes the new US government will take a series of actions to protect their domestic economy and build obstacles for Chinese sellers.（Dale Dong)
    - #Catluck, a DS webstore seller, is evaluating the solution of setting up US/EU entity for their webstores. Hoping to lower fees and protect profit. (Dale Dong)
    - #Jimi, a DTC wesbtore seller, they are still discussing how to deal with the issue of the United States increasing tariffs. Considering the need to ensure sustainable profitability, they plan to seek cost optimization from various aspects, such as through more effective tax reporting procedures. (Carmen Luo)
  - Intensified Mexican Customs Inspections Impacting Chinese Imports: Seller #Yibai reported that their containers destined for Mexico have been impounded for several days, significantly impacting their business operations. This situation arises as Mexico intensifies inspections of imports from China, influenced by additional U.S. tariffs on Chinese goods. (Frank Lin)
- [Market trend]
  - **Merchants' Coping Strategies in Response to US Tax Policy Changes: Guangzhou Weijia who sells magnetic axis****keyboards****and****home****&garden products like storage racks. For US tariff issues,**the company will absorb keyboard costs due to large profit margins. For home&garden products, transshipment via Indonesia is considered, with domestic procurement followed by shipment there. When asked about the certificate of origin, he said if unresolved, they'd directly expand in the local Indonesian market instead. (Ivy Lin)
  - **Supply Chain Strategy:**Seller #跨洲信息is concerned about the increasing US taxes on Chinese products. As a result, they are considering alternatives such asJapan, Korea, and Southeast Asia. However, they still face significant challenges in balancing the costs of overseaswarehousing, longer shipment times, and the risk ofinsufficient funds, since this model requires them to stock more inventory overseas. Reducing tax costs and finding a sustainable business model will continue to be a major headache for sellers. To seek solutions, they are consulting with numerous overseas service providers andfinancial institutionsfor advice and assistance. Sellers believe that in the coming years, there will be an upgrade in the business model. (Fabiola Li)
  - **U.S. tariff policy:**Sellers are planning onoversea warehouse due to U.S. tariff policy change. Seller#NANJINGXINMAI was consulting about solution for their U.S. company and basically this company is used for warehouse. They are under rental negotiation with property owner. And they don't want to open a bank account for this company due to tax concern.(by william)****
**Feb:**
- **[Market trend]**
  - **Merchants' Coping Strategies in Response to US Tax Policy Changes:**
    - #1. HK South Electric (a DTC merchant dealing in Print-on-Demand products) disclosed that numerous Chinese manufacturers have alreadyrelocated to the US to set up their factories. In light of this, the merchant plans to collaborate with these US-based manufacturers and conduct thefinal customization stage in the US. By doing so, the products will no longer bear the “Made-in-China” label, thereby potentially circumventing certain impacts of the US tax policy changes.  (Dale Dong)
    - #2. Zhuoda Keji (also a DTC merchant in the Print-on-Demand business)shared that the US tax policy shift is expected to lead to a cost increase. To offset this, the company intends to raise product prices marginally and then monitor the market response. Currently, they have no immediate plans to adjust its logistics or supply chain arrangements. (Nana Zhang)
- **Tariff Policy change in US impacts China merchants**:
  - Catluck, a dropshipping merchant with average order value below $50, stated they’ll adjust pricing accordingly to safeguard their profit margins. Meanwhile, they plan to register a US entity in 2025 in an attempt to cut costs. (Dale)
  - TUBCP LIMITED, also adropshippingmerchant and an Amazon seller. The owner disclosed that 100% of their products are custom-made in China. For each order worth $100, a tariff of $168will be levied. This is an extremely exorbitant amount. (Dale)
  - Catluck, a dropshipping merchant with average order value below $50, stated they’ll adjust pricing accordingly to safeguard their profit margins. Meanwhile, they plan to register aUS entity in 2025 in an attempt to cut costs. (Dale)
  - In response to the recent U.S. tax reform policy, numerous sellers remain in the wait-and-see phase. They have also inquired about the supports our company can offer.

#### Customer Voice

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331213} -->

“当时大家都崩溃了。”史文告诉《豹变》，自己合作的大大小小的货代都两眼发黑，不知道该怎么处理，几百单的货品全部积压，无法发货。
在eBay上做自发货的跨境卖家晓云告诉《豹变》，当时包括云途、顺丰等诸多物流上调了费用，宣布每单收取20元的处理费用，而她店铺的商品单价则在5到10美金，完全无法覆盖关税成本。权衡之下，第二天她就把店关了。
情况在不到72小时迎来反转，T86暂时恢复，她又把店开起来，忐忑地继续做美国生意，同时找新出路。
经历过2月那次极限“过山车”，**跨境卖家们这一次的心态因为有了大概的预期，所以相对稳定。**
另一方面，在过去的两个月中，不管卖家还是物流公司，都在尝试应对全新的关税政策，并拿出了一些权衡后的解法。多位跨境电商上下游人士对《豹变》表示，目前物流公司还未提价，但提价几乎是必然的。
只是，**不少物流公司提价的方式会改变，**不会像最初被美国政策打得措手不及时那样，每单直接收取20元的额外操作费，而是改成涨运费“双清包税（税由物流公司去付）”，对于卖家来说，心态上会更好接受。
同时，大家也在尝试走T01（标准报关）或T11（简易报关）等方式清关，物流公司会给出时效和成本上的建议。
From <[https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc](https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc)>
但现在测品成本变得很高。史文更倾向于放弃测品，依靠此前的经验，认为哪个品会火，直接备货到海外仓。“不熟悉海外消费者购买习惯的新卖家会变得更难做。”他说。
From <[https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc](https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc)>
目前，史文的生意利润率有20%到30%。在他看来，小额豁免政策一定会取消，对于卖家来说，物流成本和时效性都需要重新摸索。因此，即便是大部分使用海外仓，史文的经营成本也会不可避免地上升。
在2月的闹剧之后，他决定将价格上涨20%到30%，尽可能维持住原本的利润。
From <[https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc](https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1828537542098941756&wfr=spider&for=pc)>
原本依靠小包直邮发货的商家，能转向海外仓吗？或者放弃美国市场，转向别的市场？此前经历了关店和重开之后，晓云开始思考转型。她对《豹变》表示，如果想做海外仓，就得重开账号，因为手里的老账号没有海外仓权限。
另一方面，**无法备货到海外仓的低频或定制商品，只能依靠大幅涨价才能继续做。**

3. What is our tariff impact assessment for the US/CN plumbing fixture category?
We have conducted a cost analysis for the US/CN pilot category, plumbing fixtures, to assess the impact of the potential US/CN tariff on the sourcing country selection. As part of this, we performed a deep dive on one of the top-selling ASINs in the category (faucet B09J4GZZV) to better understand the issue. From the analysis, under the worst-case scenario of a CN>US import tax increase from 25% to 60%, the Amazon brand seller would experience a 500bps margin decrease, from 33% to 28%. Despite this, sourcing from China remains more advantageous than sourcing from Thailand, the second-largest sourcing country, due to several factors. First, although Thailand benefits from zero tariff on TH>US imports (expected to increase to 10% under Trump's new blanket tariff policy), manufacturing in Thailand is, on average, ~15% more expensive than in China, limiting the margin advantage between sourcing from China (28%) and Thailand (31%). From previous experience, we believe tariff increase can be mitigated by reducing costs, RMB devaluation and negotiating margin sharing with CN suppliers to remain competitive. For example, in 2018, when the US raised tariffs on China imports from 5% to 25%, China's share of all faucet imports still increased 700bps to 88%, and China's share of showerhead imports rose 1600bps to 86% over two years. Second, although Thailand is the second-largest faucets supply country, it primarily caters to larger OEM and ODM manufacturers with confirmed volume from large B2B buyers (e.g. Walmart). It does not have the full agile supply chain and capacity to meet the sourcing needs of SMB buyers. Based on this analysis, we believe China will remain as the best option for the pilot. We will continue to closely monitor the latest tariff changes and supply chain dynamics, making adjustments as necessary.
```

### March signpost update

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331216} -->

据国家税务总局5日消息，为支持跨境电商出口海外仓等新业态新模式发展，国家税务总局决定对纳税人以跨境电商出口海外仓方式出口的货物实行“离境即退税”。自2025年1月27日起施行。
卖家通过海外仓出口的货物，只要完成了报关离境，就可以直接申请预退税。对于尚未销售的货物，也可在货物离境后预先申报退税，待后续销售后进行税款核算。
分析认为，这一政策使得跨境电商卖家可以提前获得退税款，有利于加速资金周转，缓解现金流压力，可以更从容地应对市场变化，同时也将进一步促进海外仓的发展，吸引更多跨境电商卖家布局海外仓。
![research-march-signpost-update](assets/research/research-march-signpost-update/asset-001.png)

The same day, CBP[published a message](https://content.govdelivery.com/bulletins/gd/USDHSCBP-3d52328?wgt_ref=USDHSCBP_WIDGET_2)via the Cargo Systems Messaging Service (CSMS) providing further clarifications, including the following:
*Effective Dates & Rates:*
  - From February 4, 2025, to March 3, 2025, goods from China and Hong Kong are subject to an additional 10% duty under HTSUS classification 9903.01.20.
  - Starting on March 4, 2025, the additional duty rate increases to 20% under HTSUS classification 9903.01.24.
These duties apply in addition to all other applicable duties, taxes, fees, exactions and charges. CBP further notes that products of China that are eligible for temporary duty exemptions or reductions will be subject to the additional 20%*ad valorem*rate of duty.
*Exclusions*: Certain products are excluded from these additional duties, including donations for human suffering relief, informational materials, and goods in transit before February 1, 2025.
*Chapter 98*: Goods entered under HTSUS Chapter 98 provisions are generally exempt from these additional duties, except for specific subheadings related to repairs, alterations, or processing performed in China and Hong Kong.(repairs products originating from USA)
*FTZs*: Products from China and Hong Kong admitted into U.S. foreign trade zones (FTZs) must be classified under “privileged foreign status” and will be subject to the applicable duties upon entry for consumption.
*Drawback*: No drawback is available for these additional duties.
*De Minimis*: The Section 321*de minimis*exemption currently remains available for eligible articles.
For prior updates on these recent China tariffs, see Thompson Hine updates of[February 5, 2025](https://www.thompsonhinesmartrade.com/2025/02/cbp-issues-notice-and-csms-messages-regarding-application-of-additional-duties-on-china-and-removal-of-de-miminis-exemption/)and[February 3, 2025](https://www.thompsonhinesmartrade.com/2025/02/president-trump-initiates-tariffs-on-canada-mexico-and-china-under-the-ieepa-tariffs-on-mexico-and-canada-suspended-until-march-1-but-tariffs-on-china-start-february-4/).

### 卖家scenario assessment

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331217} -->

美国宣布对中国商品加征10%关税，对亚马逊卖家的影响可从多个维度展开分析：
---
### **一、成本结构冲击**
1. **直接成本上升**
- **采购成本**：中国制造商品（如电子产品、家居用品）的进口成本增加10%，卖家利润空间被压缩。
- **案例**：深圳某蓝牙耳机卖家原成本$10/件，关税后增至$11，若售价维持$15，毛利率从50%降至36%。
2. **供应链调整压力**
- **替代生产地**：部分卖家转向越南（人工成本比中国低30%）、墨西哥（美墨加协定免税）设厂，但需承担$5万-$20万的初期迁移成本。
- **分散风险**：头部卖家将订单拆分至中国（60%）、东南亚（30%）、其他地区（10%）。
---
### **二、定价策略与市场竞争**
1. **价格传导能力**
- **必需品**：如手机配件（充电线、保护壳），需求弹性低，卖家可将80%-100%关税成本转嫁给消费者。
- **非必需品**：如装饰品，涨价15%可能导致销量下降25%（数据参考2020年关税影响）。
2. **平台内卷加剧**
- **小卖家出清**：利润率低于15%的卖家或被迫退出，预计10%-20%中小卖家受冲击。
- **大卖家优势**：Anker等品牌凭借规模效应（采购成本低5%-8%）和海外仓（规避部分关税）巩固份额。
---
### **三、运营模式重构**
1. **库存策略调整**
- **提前囤货**：在关税生效前3个月，亚马逊FBA仓中国商品入库量激增40%（参考2019年数据）。
- **海外仓扩容**：卖家在美西仓库库存量占比从50%提升至65%，缩短交货周期至3天。
2. **合规成本增加**
- **原产地认证**：申请非中国原产地证明（如越南）需增加$500-$2000/单的合规费用。
- **关税分类争议**：聘请专业清关公司（费用$1000/月）以避免HS编码误判导致的罚款。
---
### **四、长期生态演变**
1. **供应链多元化**
- **产业转移**：预计未来3年，中国占亚马逊美国站商品比例从70%降至55%，东南亚份额升至25%。
- **近岸制造**：墨西哥对美出口的亚马逊商品年增长30%（2023年数据）。
2. **平台政策适配**
- **算法权重调整**：亚马逊A10算法可能降低涨价商品排名，倒逼卖家优化广告投放（CPC成本上升20%）。
- **物流合作深化**：与UPS/FedEx谈判运费折扣，对冲关税成本（大卖家可获5%-10%折扣）。
---
### **五、应对策略矩阵**
| **策略类型** | **适用卖家** | **实施路径** | **风险提示** |
|-------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------------------------|------------------------------|
| **成本转嫁** |强品牌溢价能力|分阶段涨价（每月5%），同步优化产品页面|竞品低价冲击导致份额流失|
| **供应链迁移** |年营收>$500万|越南试点生产（首单1万件），保留中国备用线|品控风险，交货延迟罚款|
| **品类优化** |多SKU卖家|砍掉关税敏感品（如家具），聚焦低影响类目|库存积压需清仓（折扣30%-50%）|
| **税务对冲** |财税合规基础完善|利用中美关税排除清单（如部分纺织品）|政策变动导致申报失效|
| **本土化运营** |目标美国本土制造|收购美国小型工厂（$50万-$200万），生产高附加值产品|劳工成本激增（中国3倍）|
---
### **结语：危局中的进化契机**
加征关税虽冲击短期利润，但将加速亚马逊生态的深度重构：
- **优胜劣汰**：淘汰低效卖家，提升行业集中度（TOP10卖家市占率或从35%升至45%）；
- **创新驱动**：倒逼产品升级（如智能家居集成AI功能），摆脱价格战泥潭；
- **全球布局**：成熟卖家借势构建“中国研发+多国制造+全球销售”的韧性网络。
最终，能快速适应关税新常态的卖家，将在洗牌后的市场中占据更有利生态位。
From <[https://www.wearesellers.com/question/101786](https://www.wearesellers.com/question/101786)>

Vietnam Won Big in Donald Trump’s First Trade War. Now, It’s a Target.
Story by Liza Lin, Jason Douglas, Rebecca Feng
![research-vn-wsj-article](assets/research/research-vn-wsj-article/asset-001.jpg)
Vietnam, a haven for Chinese companies eager to skirt U.S. tariffs, also benefits from a young and cheap labor force.© Thanh Hue for WSJ
Vietnam found the sweet spot in the global economy during President-elect Donald Trump’s [first trade war](https://www.wsj.com/economy/trade/china-coped-with-trumps-first-trade-war-a-second-one-will-be-tougher-8e9e3e21) with Beijing: smack in the middle of the U.S. and China.
The country became a magnet for Chinese manufacturers looking for a production base from which to ship their goods to the U.S. tariff-free.
Now, as the incoming administration girds for[new battles over trade](https://www.wsj.com/economy/trade/trump-tariff-rates-china-world-trade-charts-3d6aee09), Trump and his team are signaling that they intend to slam this backdoor shut.
Such a move would hurt Vietnam’s small but rapidly growing economy, and likely mean higher prices for U.S. consumers who buy Vietnamese goods and[U.S. companies](https://www.wsj.com/economy/trade/american-companies-are-stocking-up-to-get-ahead-of-trumps-china-tariffs-c1ca4744)supplied by Vietnamese factories. Since Trump placed tariffs on Chinese goods six years ago, Vietnam has expanded around 8% a year, buoyed by a gusher of foreign investment and booming exports to the U.S.
The country now supplies a third of the sports shoes, half of the wooden beds and dining tables, and a quarter of the solar cells imported by the U.S.
Outside its capital Hanoi, once-sleepy northern provinces have been turned into export powerhouses, with rice paddies giving way to hulking billion-dollar factories assembling smartphones and semiconductors. U.S. companies with suppliers in Vietnam include Apple, Nike and Gap.
But in May, Jamieson Greer, Trump’s nominee for U.S. trade representative, said the U.S. should tighten trade rules to prevent what he described as “third-country workarounds,” in which goods that contain a lot of Chinese parts or were made in a third country by a subsidiary of a Chinese company enter the U.S. without facing the steep tariffs they would have done had they come direct from China. Though he didn’t name them specifically, Vietnam and Mexico are among those countries where Chinese companies have built bridgeheads into the U.S. market.
Vietnam is a trade target of the new administration in other ways, too. The president-elect has floated the idea of levying tariffs on all imports into the U.S., and talks about forcing countries to close their trade gaps with the U.S.
Vietnam exports to the U.S. nine times as much as what it imports from the country, putting it fourth behind China, Mexico and the European Union for U.S.’s biggest bilateral trade deficits. In 2019, Trump said Vietnam was the “single worst abuser of everybody” on trade, a charge Hanoi denies.
From <[https://www.msn.com/en-us/money/markets/vietnam-won-big-in-donald-trump-s-first-trade-war-now-it-s-a-target/ar-AA1vVfpR?ocid=BingNewsSerp](https://www.msn.com/en-us/money/markets/vietnam-won-big-in-donald-trump-s-first-trade-war-now-it-s-a-target/ar-AA1vVfpR?ocid=BingNewsSerp)>
“Given Vietnam’s large trade surplus with the U.S., the country may be particularly exposed to a more protectionist economic agenda,” said Adam Sitkoff, head of the American Chamber of Commerce in Hanoi.
Still, some in Vietnam say Trump’s [focus on China](https://www.wsj.com/economy/trade/chinas-nightmare-a-second-trade-war-with-trump-363313ae) and Mexico will allow Vietnam to fly under the radar, ultimately driving more foreign investment into the country. And any new tariffs on Vietnamese goods will likely be lower than those Trump levies on China, helping Vietnam maintain its competitive edge.
“A lot of the things that made Vietnam attractive during Trump’s first term are still the same,” said Thuy Anh Nguyen, a country specialist at Dragon Capital, a Ho Chi Minh City-based asset manager, citing improved infrastructure and low labor and other costs.
For some tech companies especially, Vietnam is still a big draw. U.S. chip juggernaut Nvidia said this month that it plans to open an artificial intelligence research-and-development center in Vietnam
Samsung, the South Korean consumer-electronics giant that has invested more than $22 billion in the communist country, recently agreed to construct a $1.8 billion new factory making diode displays.
Apple has about 35 suppliers with production in Vietnam, triple the number it had there before the trade war began. Apple, which began diversifying its supply chain from China in recent years, now assembles iPads, MacBooks and its earphones in the Southeast Asian country.
Vietnam’s success in straddling the U.S. and China reflects its favorable demographics and geography. A country of 98 million with a young and cheap labor force, its border with China allows easy shipments of materials and parts to factories. Vietnam has also been[diplomatically adept](https://www.wsj.com/world/why-everyone-wants-to-be-vietnams-friend-d23b6881)in its dealings with Washingon and Beijing, something its former Communist Party head, Nguyen Phu Trong, described as “bamboo diplomacy”—foreign relations that are firmly rooted, but flexible.
In November, U.S. companies including fashion designer Steve Madden and Acushnet, the maker of Titleist golf balls and FootJoy golf shoes, talked in investor calls about moving shoe production to Vietnam from China. Acushnet said it would make all of its shoes in Vietnamese factories by the end of 2025, instead of in China.
Since Trump’s first term began in 2016, Vietnam has attracted almost $290 billion in foreign investment, almost as much as it pulled in over the previous two decades. South Korea and Japan are among its biggest investors, but China has been rapidly catching up. China and Hong Kong have invested $54 billion there in the same period, 10 times as much as U.S.-based companies.
Singapore is another big investor, pumping in around $58 billion over the same period. Corporate filings suggest that some Singapore-based investment might originate from China. Chinese manufacturers typically set up subsidiaries in Singapore or Hong Kong to funnel money into Vietnam, according to stock filings, mainly to minimize taxes.
UE Chairs, a Chinese maker of ergonomic chairs for retailers such as Staples and Office Depot, built its first Vietnam factory in late 2018, as tariffs on Chinese furniture spiked to 25%. It set up a wholly owned Hong Kong subsidiary and used that entity to invest in the Vietnamese factory. Since then, UE Chairs has added another plant in Vietnam with a third on the way. Now, almost all of its U.S. exports come from Vietnam, and Vietnam has helped the company expand its market share in America, the company has said.
Chinese socks and apparel maker Zhejiang Jasan Holding Group, which counts Uniqlo, Under Armour and Gap as its biggest clients, announced plans in October to build a new factory in northern Vietnam’s Nam Dinh province. Jasan made the investment through its wholly owned subsidiary in Singapore, according to stock exchange filings.
Jasan’s overseas assets, which the company says are mainly composed of its production bases in Vietnam, more than doubled between 2017, before the onset of the U.S.-China trade war, and 2023, according to its annual reports. Overseas assets now account for more than 40% of Jasan’s total assets.
Even if Trump were to put in higher tariffs, factories take years to set up. Companies would rather divert exports away from the U.S. to other markets than move out, said Frank Vossen, whose company Seditex helps foreign businesses source and set up in Vietnam.
Vossen said the number of new inquiries he received from American clients doubled this year from a year ago. Many were sourcing metal and plastic parts. Existing U.S. customers are also contemplating moving more business to Vietnam, he said.
There are some signs of caution. Since Trump’s election win, some Taiwanese electronics manufacturers have started reconsidering possible new investments in Vietnam, fearing that his administration will put tariffs on Vietnamese exports or target the country for other alleged trade violations.
Vietnam also has problems with corruption and a shortage of skilled manpower. And it still relies heavily on components and intermediate goods from China. This lack of an ecosystem for components has sent business looking into neighboring countries such as Thailand, which have more local raw material manufacturing, or sometimes even back to China.
Jake Phipps, CEO of furniture, toilet fixtures and lighting supplier Phipps International, shut a Vietnam factory that made medicine cabinets and mirrors because he couldn’t find reliable local parts suppliers and had to import everything from China.
With excess industrial capacity in China, prices there have come down, and he has moved some production back there instead, said Phipps.
“China’s quality is way superior at the end of the day,” he added.

Our latest economic forecasts assume Trump will impose a 30% across-the-board tariff on Chinese imports into the US, a 25% tariff on selected goods from Europe including steel, aluminium, and automobiles, and a 10% tariff on selected goods in Canada, Mexico, Japan, South Korea, and Vietnam. This would take the effective tariff rate on US goods imports to around 6% from 2%.
From <[https://www.oxfordeconomics.com/resource/trump-tariff-a-comprehensive-research-report/](https://www.oxfordeconomics.com/resource/trump-tariff-a-comprehensive-research-report/)>
In its latest year-ahead outlook, Morgan Stanley has assumed a gradual rise in tariffs on imports from China, expecting an increase in tariff rates through the next few years. The investment bank anticipates a phased implementation starting in the first quarter of 2025, with the weighted average tariff on all Chinese imports increasing from the current 10 per cent to 26 per cent by the end of 2025, and reaching 36 per cent by the end of 2026.
From <[https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/international/business/several-tariff-tidal-wave-may-hit-china-as-trump-threatens-round-2-of-trade-war-morgan-stanley-makes-predictions/articleshow/115717971.cms?from=mdr](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/international/business/several-tariff-tidal-wave-may-hit-china-as-trump-threatens-round-2-of-trade-war-morgan-stanley-makes-predictions/articleshow/115717971.cms?from=mdr)>
Kilcrease said that Trump’s team has “unfinished business” from his first term in office because “they did not get everything they wanted” from the[Phase One trade deal](https://ustr.gov/sites/default/files/files/agreements/phase%20one%20agreement/US_China_Agreement_Fact_Sheet.pdf)with China that was signed in 2020. “Since they didn’t get everything they wanted, they’re going to have to ratchet up the pressure here,” she said. “So, I do think the60% tariffs are something that are likely going to be an early administration action.”
According to[an analysis](https://taxpolicycenter.org/taxvox/tpc-trump-tariffs-would-raise-household-taxes-and-slow-imports)by the nonpartisan Tax Policy Center, a basic10% tariff, plus a targeted tariff of 60%on Chinese goods, would increase net federal revenues by about$2.8 trillionover a decade.
As Yahoo! Finance’s Washington correspondent, Ben Werschkul,[noted](https://finance.yahoo.com/news/trumps-trade-rhetoric-may-not-be-all-bluster-he-needs-mammoth-tariffs-to-pay-for-everything-else-he-wants-184020428.html)in October, “Donald Trump is making dramatic new tariff pronouncements almost daily that one could be tempted to dismiss as bluster, but it is increasingly clear that he needs a titanic level of duties to fulfill the promises of his second-term agenda.”
He added: “Indeed,[a detailed analysis of Trump’s plans](https://www.crfb.org/papers/fiscal-impact-harris-and-trump-campaign-plans)from the nonpartisan Committee for a Responsible Federal Budget found that one of the only sizable offsets to his over $10 trillion in campaign trail promises is tariffs — which are projected to raiseabout $2.7 trillion.”
On the other hand, in October, a team of experts at the Center for Strategic & International Studies[made the case](https://www.csis.org/analysis/making-tariffs-great-again-does-president-trump-have-legal-authority-implement-new-tariffs)that Trump does have the ability to set a wide range of tariffs, includinga tax of up to 20%on imported products.
“As of today, there are multiple legal authorities that Trump could rely on to justify the imposition of increased tariffs, including many that Trump already availed himself of during his presidency,” the CSIS team wrote. “These include Sections 232 and 301, the International Emergency Economic Powers Act (IEEPA), Section 122 Balance-of-Payments Authority, and Section 338 of the Tariff Act of 1930. While Section 232 requires an investigation by the Department of Commerce and Section 301 requires an investigation and determination by the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative (USTR), these procedural niceties could be accomplished in relatively short order by cabinet officials, particularly since undue delay could put them at risk of getting fired.”

为支持新型业态发展，我们出台的《政策措施》中提出了具体措施。下一步，我们将持续加大工作力度。
  - 一是加强供需对接，就是要组织在跨境电商平台上开店的这些外贸企业和在境外的这些智慧物流平台进行对接，支持有条件的地方举办跨境电商交易会、供需对接会。
  - 二是做好合规建设，现在我国跨境电商的出口企业已经有12万家，这些企业大多数是小企业，它在做电商出口的过程中会遇到报关问题、税务问题、产品质量问题、知识产权问题，所以要帮助这些企业搞培训，提高他们的合规能力。
  - 三是大力发展跨境电商赋能产业带。鼓励平台企业、链主企业深入地方特色产业带，包括全国的165个跨境电商综试区进行合作，帮助更多外贸企业特别是中小微企业借助跨境电商的线上平台接触到国外采购商，拿到订单，参与国际贸易。
  - 四是深化国际合作。目前中国已经签署的自由贸易协定有23个，有的签得比较早。在最近签的双边自贸协定，或者正在升级的过去的自贸协定中，都把跨境电商作为一个新的条款纳入到双边自贸协定中

#### Financial times article

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331226} -->

  - [https://www.ft.com/content/bd3a0377-58a9-4283-b9c4-d7445b7d3df8](https://www.ft.com/content/bd3a0377-58a9-4283-b9c4-d7445b7d3df8)
  - A tit-for-tat between China and the US on trade this month hints at what could well lie ahead if Donald Trump delivers on campaign promises to up the ante on Chinese tariffs when he returns to the White House.
In a long overdue move, the US has just updated its export sanctions on China, focusing on high-bandwidth memory chips and semiconductor manufacturing equipment. Washington also added another 140 Chinese companies to the commerce department’s so-called “entity list”, effectively making it very difficult for those US companies to supply technology to them.
As has been the case since 2018, China has been quick to counterpunch, in this case by banning or limiting US purchases of several critical minerals while tightening controls on graphite. China’s retaliatory action is a surgical strike with important strategic consequences for key US industries, ranging from semiconductors and satellites to infrared technology and fibre optic cables, to lithium batteries and solar cells. These actions are comparable to what Washington is seeking with its “small yard, high fence” strategy aimed at restricting access to critical US technologies.
It is a reminder that retaliation is the high-octane fuel of conflict escalation. This is not well understood in US policy circles that seem to harbour the mistaken notion of a one-way dependency — that China is uniquely beholden to external demand and new technologies from the US. This leaves out the other half of the equation. The US is also heavily reliant on low-cost Chinese goods to make ends meet for income-constrained consumers; the US needs Chinese surplus saving to help fill its void of domestic saving; and US producers rely on China as America’s third-largest export market. This codependency means the US depends on China just as much China depends on America.
Trump doesn’t buy this logic. During Trump 1.0, US tariffs on Chinese products were raised from 3 per cent in 2016 to 19 per cent by 2020. Trump held the mistaken view that there was a bilateral China fix for a multilateral trade deficit with 106 countries.
That backfired. Over the subsequent years, the overall US merchandise trade deficit widened from $879bn 2018 to $1.06tn in 2023. Predictably, in response to tariffs, the Chinese share of the overall US trade deficit fell from 47 to 26 per cent over this same five-year period.
However, the Chinese portion was simply diverted to Mexico, Vietnam, Canada, Korea, Taiwan, India, Ireland and Germany. And it turns out more than 70 per cent of the trade diversion away from China went to higher-cost or comparable-cost nations, underscoring that trade diversion is the equivalent of a tax rise on US companies and consumers.
Expect more of the same in a second Trump administration. And as US actions escalate, retaliation from China will probably broaden. For example, China’s latest actions on critical minerals open up the possibility of wide-ranging constraints on rare earths, which are of enormous strategic importance to the US.
Then, of course, there is the ultimate financial weapon — Greater China’s $1tn in direct holdings of US Treasury securities (including $772bn by the PRC and $233bn by Hong Kong as of September 2024). Cavalier Americans typically dismiss this possibility, claiming China wouldn’t dare flirt with this nuclear option because it would hurt them more than us.
Oh really? There are a couple of “bad dream” options to consider: China could go on a buyer’s strike during upcoming Treasury auctions, or, even more extreme, it could start to unload its outsize position as America’s second-largest foreign creditor. Either option would be devastating for America’s deficit-prone economy and would unleash havoc in the US bond market, with wrenching collateral damage in world financial markets. While it seems far-fetched, almost suicidal, for China to spark such a financial meltdown, it is equally reckless to dismiss the “tail risk” consequences of a trapped adversary.
Much of the post-election policy discussion has focused on tariff initiatives likely to be forthcoming in Trump 2.0. Sino-American codependency urges us to think less about unilateral actions and more about the retaliatory responses to those actions. Trump’s nationalistic view of “America First” ignores how much a saving-short US economy depends on China for goods and financial capital. China has plenty of “Trump cards” to send a very different message.

#### Reuters article

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331227} -->

Weaker Chinese yuan talk raises spectre of FX race to the bottom
By[Tom Westbrook](https://www.reuters.com/authors/tom-westbrook/)and[Alun John](https://www.reuters.com/authors/alun-john/)
December 12, 20246:59 AM GMT+8Updated 12 days ago
![research-reuters-article](assets/research/research-reuters-article/asset-001.jpg)
Item 1 of 2 Signs of Chinese yuan and U.S. dollar are seen at a currency exchange store in Shanghai, China August 8, 2019. REUTERS/Aly Song/File Photo
[1/2]Signs of Chinese yuan and U.S. dollar are seen at a currency exchange store in Shanghai, China August 8, 2019. REUTERS/Aly Song/File Photo[Purchase Licensing Rights, opens new tab](https://www.reutersagency.com/en/licensereuterscontent/?utm_medium=rcom-article-media&utm_campaign=rcom-rcp-lead)
- Summary
- Reuters reports China considering a weaker yuan on trade threats
- Depreciation highlights broader FX implications of tariffs, economist says
- Yuan slips, Aussie dollar hits 1-yr low
SINGAPORE/LONDON, Dec 11 (Reuters) - High-level discussions in China about allowing its currency to weaken next year underscore the risk for investors and companies that big foreign exchange moves are coming as U.S. tariffs shift global trade and money flows, analysts said.
[Reuters reported](https://www.reuters.com/markets/currencies/chinese-authorities-are-considering-weaker-yuan-trump-trade-risks-loom-sources-2024-12-11/)on Wednesday that China was considering letting the yuan fall to weather what is likely to be a sharp hike in tariffs, citing people familiar with the matter. The yuan immediately dipped against the dollar, along with currencies across Asia which are highly sensitive to Chinese demand.
Advertisement · Scroll to continue
While a weaker yuan had been widely expected, with pressure on the exchange rate since the election of Donald Trump as U.S. president, framing it as a policy shift may herald the start of a new round of global tariffs, trade tensions and currency intervention.
"Currency adjustments are on the table as a tool to be used to mitigate the effects of tariffs. I think that is clear," said Fred Neumann, chief Asia economist at HSBC in Hong Kong.
Advertisement · Scroll to continue
"Taking the currency weaker might be a signal by China to the rest of the world that there are exchange rate implications of imposing tariffs."
A cheaper exchange rate helps exporters by making their prices more competitive internationally.
The yuan dipped about 0.3% and as far as 7.2803 to the dollar after the Reuters report. The Australian dollar , which is sensitive to moves in the yuan given its hefty commodity exports, touched a one-year low.
Trump has said he plans to impose a 10% universal tariff on imports to the U.S. and a 60% tariff on Chinese goods.
Financial markets have been bracing for more volatility from his inauguration on Jan. 20, but have been unsure how seriously to take his threats.
Reuters spoke to three people who have knowledge of the discussions about letting the yuan weaken, one of whom said the central bank had considered a fall to about 7.5 to the dollar - roughly a 3.5% depreciation from current levels around 7.25.
Still, that is at the weaker end of investment bank expectations, adding to a sense among[investors](https://www.reuters.com/business/investors-say-they-know-how-trade-trump-20-2024-11-26/)that China is determined to be better prepared for trade shocks this time around.
"If they need to revitalise the economy, and they tend to be more interested on focusing on exports, there is quite a compelling logic that they may allow the renminbi to soften," said Jane Foley, head of currency strategy at Rabobank.
INTENSE, FAST
A complicating factor for China is where any slide would leave the yuan relative to non-dollar currencies, especially in Asia where many neighbours such as Vietnam have grown as hubs for finishing Chinese manufactured goods and avoiding U.S. sanctions.
Rong Ren Goh, a portfolio manager in the fixed income team at Eastspring Investments, said he expects China will orchestrate a controlled and gradual depreciation but "Asian currencies, particularly those of export-driven economies, are likely to adjust in tandem with the yuan on a trade-weighted basis."
China's exporters have been[hoarding dollars](https://www.reuters.com/markets/asia/corporate-china-seeks-dollars-trade-tensions-rise-2024-11-21/)with an eye on a rate of 7.5 as a point to start selling, but they have also been seeking ways to avoid taking currency risks altogether by invoicing in yuan and other such workarounds - especially as the yuan has gained this year on peers[(.CFSCNYI), opens new tab](https://www.reuters.com/markets/quote/.CFSCNYI).
"If China takes the currency aggressively lower, it raises the risk of a tariff cascade," said HSBC's Neumann, if it prompts other economies to put up their own levies to protect their industrial base from extremely cheap Chinese imports.
"It could lead to a backlash among other trading partners, and that's not in the interest of China."
To be sure, much of the risk lies in the speed or shock value of any U.S. move, and some market participants don't expect Trump will be in a rush to take direct action.
"There’s some voices in markets calling for a quick 10-20% depreciation (in the yuan) to help offset tariffs," said ING's Greater China economist Lynn Song.
"We don’t expect an intentional and sharp depreciation like this as it will be ineffective to counteract tariffs, given this could easily be categorized by the U.S. as currency manipulation and result in further tariff hikes."
Still, at recent analysts' briefings in Singapore, Trump's trade policy was seen as a true wildcard and a weaker Chinese currency was the consensus for analysts at Nomura and MUFG.
"My view is that there will be FX flexibility that comes through," said Craig Chan, head of global currency strategy at Nomura, before Reuters' report on China's forex discussions.
He recommended a few long dollar positions in Asia.
"Long dollar/CNH is one. We have a target of 7.60 by the end of May. It could be intense, could be fast," he said. "That would clearly be the risk to dollar/China - moving higher, faster."
And at MUFG, a forecast for a drop to 7.5 per dollar was predicated on the assumption of an average 40% tariff on Chinese goods.
"A 60% tariff on China products would require a 10%-12% yuan depreciation against the dollar (since September) to 7.8 or beyond ... everything else being equal," MUFG analysts said.
During Trump's first term as president, the yuan weakened more than 12% against the dollar during a series of tit-for-tat tariff announcements between March 2018 and May 2020.

China's 'hidden' subsidies fuel export onslaught
Total annual tax refunds to companies rise 400% over 10 years
YUSHO CHO, Nikkei staff writer
October 13, 2024 16:22 JST
TOKYO -- China is ramping up its use of tax refunds and other "hidden" subsidies for[electric vehicles](https://asia.nikkei.com/Business/Automobiles/Electric-vehicles)and other strategic businesses in an effort to give them a competitive edge in world markets, according to many experts. China's total annual tax rebates to major mainland companies rose 400% over the decade up to 2023, fueling their export to the U.S. and Europe.
The rebates come on top of substantial direct subsidies that the government of Chinese President Xi Jinping allocates to key industries.

**第一、二轮清单主要针对高新技术产品，第三、四轮清单广泛涉及中间品、资本品和消费品。**前两轮加税主要针对我国制造强国行动纲领《中国制造2025》中提到的高新技术产品：主要涉及通用机械、专用设备、电子产品、汽车、家电、通信设备、医疗器械、航空设备、化工、钢铁等。后两轮清单商品涉及面较广，除了中间品和资本品之外，还涵盖了纺织服装、箱包家具、食品饮料、农林牧渔等消费品。**未加征关税的商品主要分布在消费品领域**，以减轻对消费者的影响，包括部分农产品、消费电子产品、医疗设备和药品、环保产品、基础设施材料等
清单四只有7.5%关税，增速慢，不受影响
Response:
  - 清单一和清单二均出现了明显的“抢出口”
  - 美国进口增长地区与我国出口增长国家高度重合，如墨西哥、加拿大、越南等，意味着这些国家大概率是中国商品通过转口贸易、简单加工贴牌等方式绕道进入美国的中转站
  - Consumer goods not influenced much, until 2023
  - 人民币兑美元汇率由2017年末的6.51贬至2019年末的6.96，贬值幅度超过6%

### 发货模式

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331235} -->

国内自发货、Drop shipping(无货源发货)、亚马逊FBA发货、虚拟海外仓发货，以及海外仓一件代发。每一种发货方式都有自身的优势和不足，卖家应该如何选择，需要了解对比后心中方有底
价格低廉的专线小包或优质外邮小包的自发货方式，会更贴合铺货型独立站卖家的需要
精品垂直品类的独立站卖家，在发货时会优先考虑时效性、走货可靠性、售后退换货便利性等物流品质因素。所以，该类型卖家采用优质的第三方海外仓
独立站大卖家在发货方式上选择第三方海外仓发货最为合适
中小型卖家：可以先采用国内自发货、Drop shipping(无货源发货)，以及虚拟海外仓发货这几种方式，进行前期的测试

### 清关流程

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331236} -->

**2、关税征收方法解释：**
1）价值低于800美元(含800美元)：免税。
2）价值在800-2500美元之间(含2500美元):非正式报关(Informal)；只需缴纳关税Dutiable(无需缴纳货物处理费)；
3）货值超过2500美元:正式报关(Formal):需要交关税Dutiable+货物处理费MPF(注:反倾销产品和反补贴产品的计算方法与上述不同)
**3、关税计算公式：**
MPF=FOBX货物运营税率(税率一般为0.3464%，MPF最低25.67美元，最高497美元)
FOB Price：货物价值。
From <[https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_finance_tax/34-49066.html](https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_finance_tax/34-49066.html)>
美国关税可从价（货值的百分比），也可从量（单位美元/分）计征。关税税率在0%和37.5%之间，平均关税税率为5.63%。
From <[https://www.cnabke.com/school/content/64ad07e0fbeacc27ff404750.html](https://www.cnabke.com/school/content/64ad07e0fbeacc27ff404750.html)>
FBA -
  1. 亚马逊是不负责清关和中国派送至亚马逊仓库的服务的，FBA头程服务就是指从中国将货物运至亚马逊仓库，有专门的服务可以实现这个需求，比如在华南、华东都有服务的铭邦国际
  1. 亚马逊不作为清关主体，不负责清关和缴税，一定要做好申报和关税预付并且提前准备好当地清关进口商。一般跨境物流公司如铭邦国际是可以提供税号借用的
  1. 货物在进入亚马逊FBA仓之前与亚马逊无任何关系。所以亚马逊不作为货物进口方，不承担货物因进口清关和进口产生的关税等一切费用，如果在进口过程中产生关税亚马逊是没有实际的收件人去处理的。所以为了避免退货风险，各快递公司都会建议做DDP(关税由发件人预付)或者用第三方清关代理人作为实际收货人
From <[https://www.amz123.com/ask/ELpQs2oE](https://www.amz123.com/ask/ELpQs2oE)>
  1. 货代模式：自清关、清关包税报一个总价
  1. 低申报模式和风险（发票上低开价格）
[http://www.kwt56.com/notice.php?id=326](http://www.kwt56.com/notice.php?id=326)
**那对FBA卖家的影响呢？**
没什么影响。
我们发送FBA货件的时候，你找的货代公司基本都是用自己的进口商，是把货柜作为一个整体来进行清关，虽然里面也会有关税产生，但这个都不需要卖家去操心。
所以，上面这封邮件提醒的对象主要是**FBM自发货卖家（T86小包类和快递类）**，自发货的卖家在以后申报时，不超过800美金就可以继续免税。
From
要使亚马逊物流货件符合321条款的入境要求，商品所有者必须在装运单或报关申报文件中提供他们的姓名或其公司名称。 使用以下格式可满足美国海关的要求：
[您的法定名称] c/o亚马逊物流
运营中心地址
From <[https://www.wearesellers.com/question/95848](https://www.wearesellers.com/question/95848)>
灰色清关是指通过非正规或半合法的途径进行货物清关，通常是为了减少关税和费用。
灰清的话，可以很好地规避费用，通过虚报价格、品名、数量等方式，减少应缴纳的关税和费用。灰清就是通过各种手段减少了关税和增值税，成本降低。而且所需文件不齐全，或者文件内容不真实，直接自己操作一份。手续相对简化，清关速度较快。不过便捷的同时也存在风险，由于操作的不合规，容易被海关查验、扣押，甚至面临罚款。
From <[https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1814143773115450893&wfr=spider&for=pc](https://baijiahao.baidu.com/s?id=1814143773115450893&wfr=spider&for=pc)>
(1)虚拟进口人，很多货代通过在境外注册大量税号或空壳公司作为名义进口商，来帮卖家做进口清关，空壳公司没有实际交易，经不起税务审查。而且以代理商或[海外仓](https://global.lianlianpay.com/tags/Overseas_warehouses)名义替国内的卖家进口货物，若清关出了问题，则容易影响其他客人的货物清关，即使参考货值来补税和交罚金也可能损失巨大。在海关，货权属于税号的提供方，出了问题的货物，卖家也无法主张货权。
(2)低报避税，或将商品归类在零税率中，整个报关过程涉及很多单据造假的问题，其实若申报数据经常不符或申报单价过低，会严重影响到进口商在海关的信誉，严重时会被海关处罚及停止进口资质。正规的货运代理及进口清关公司就会比较谨慎，在正式清关时不仅必须有代理报关委托书POA( Power of Attorney)，还会要求上游代理同意进口商和报关行有权修正低价及不符合的商品价格，不低于网上销售的最低价申报，品名一致，关税税率以最终报关时海关判定的税率为准，关税由卖家无条件承担，以便顺利清关。
(3)拆包，这是物流商利用低价进口免税优惠政策，把一票大货拆成几票免税额以下的小件货，提供虚假进口人身份IOR去规避正式报关的方式，以免缴进口税。海关的检验力度具有季节性，在年底货运旺季，各国海关都会加强排查。这就是为什么[FBA](https://global.lianlianpay.com/tags/fba_intro)只接受卖家自发头程，撇清了进口环节的清关及税务问题。
From <[https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_logistics/17-89588.html](https://global.lianlianpay.com/article_logistics/17-89588.html)>
![research-3](assets/research/research-3/asset-001.png)
1、T11
这个模式申报价是销售价，正常的税费远高于T01。目前这个渠道的反馈是业内有物流公司继续用双清包税的玩法，不收 2.62 美金的操作费。
这个模式的优势是如果遇到查验，海关只会扣下被查且有问题的货件，其他的都能正常发货，缺点是时效比较差，大概需要 20 多天。
2、T01+海外仓换单
这是最传统简单的清关方式，要搭配海外仓换单来操作，比较少出问题。有些操作会控制品类，采用中性面单，到仓后，再换单。时效快，10天左右，但遇到查验，有一个违规，整批货全部扣下，连带比较严重，而且品名限制比较严格。
From <[https://www.cifnews.com/article/173005](https://www.cifnews.com/article/173005)>

### 符合原产地规则

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331237} -->

根据海关总署 2018年修订的《关于非优惠原产地规则中实质性改变标准的规定》，其中“实质性改变”的具体标准，和世界贸易组织的《原产地规则协定》(Agreement on Rules of Origin)中给的认定标准是一致的，具体包括：
(1)基本标准——税则归类改变：是指在该国家或地区对来源于其他国家或地区的原产材料进行制造、加工后，所得货物在《中华人民共和国进出口税则)中的四位数级税目归类发生了变化；
(2)补充标准之一——“制造、加工工序”标准：是指在该国家或地区进行的赋予制造、加工后所得货物基本特征的主要工序；
(3)补充标准之二——“从价百分比”标准：是指在该国家或地区对非该国家或地区的原产材料进行制造、加工后的增值部分超过了所得货物价值的 30%。
值得注意的是，并非所有的货物都可以使用补充标准。仅在《适用制造或者加工工序及从价百分比标准的货物清单》中列明的货物可以适用以制造、加工工序和从价百分比的补充标准，并且应该按照该清单中列明的条件判断是否发生了“实质性改变”。例如对《中华人民共和国进出口税则》第43章43.04的人造毛皮制品，在判断“实质性改变”时可以采用制造、加工工序的补充标准，并以“裁剪、缝制”作为该标准的判断条件。
From <[https://global.lianlianpay.com/article/MTA0MTAzLDkzZQ.html](https://global.lianlianpay.com/article/MTA0MTAzLDkzZQ.html)>
2. **合规成本增加**
- **原产地认证**：申请非中国原产地证明（如越南）需增加$500-$2000/单的合规费用。
- **关税分类争议**：聘请专业清关公司（费用$1000/月）以避免HS编码误判导致的罚款。

### Oversea supply chain + tariff sensitivity

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331239} -->

<u>**东兴证券**</u>
海外建厂方面，乐歌股份最早于2015年已经在越南投资建设生产基地，致欧科技、赛维时代等公司2024年开始着手供应链海外迁移工作，具有成本优势的东南亚仍旧是首选地区。
美国的劳动力市场不支持全部的产业链转移，尤其是劳动密集型产业很难实现，比如纺织服装、家具等

#### Mechanism

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331240} -->

此前有卖家表示可以通过东南亚转运的方式降低关税，现在看来这条路径也是比较难了。
东南亚地区的加税情况是这样的：越南(46%)、泰国(36%)、印度尼西亚（32%）、马来西亚（24%）、菲律宾（17%）。
美国这个表单让我们看到了四个字“严防死守”。
大家如果非要转运的话，墨西哥和加拿大还有点机会：
**墨西哥转运路径：深圳 → 墨西哥 → 贴标换单 → 陆路进美国**
**这条路径成本增加约8%，胜在操作简单，但需要稳定的墨西哥组装厂商合作。**
**加拿大转运路径：深圳 → 加拿大仓 → 美东/美西分仓**
**这条路径增加的成本较高，适合高客单产品，需要原产地切换+仓储中转，无法依靠深圳一条链发货。**
From <[https://www.cifnews.com/article/173021](https://www.cifnews.com/article/173021)>

#### Destinations

<!-- OneNote page ID: {E0A1CC49-A7F4-03C4-1C9B-A0D21C7657D9}{1}{E1845805134490290339618856254607888248331241} -->

**哪些企业选择出海越南？**
早在2000年前，就有台企在越南设厂。以裕元集团为例，该集团曾是东莞最大的鞋厂，但随着时间推移，裕元的产能逐渐转移到越南，导致东莞厂区人员大幅减少。除了鞋类、纺织、家具等行业，近年来，电子企业成为出海越南的主力军。超过25家苹果手机供应商在越南设厂，三星手机的全球销售中，有超过50%是“越南制造”的产品。此外，东莞的企业如立讯精密、长盈精密等也在越南设厂，使越南成为仅次于中国的全球第二大智能手机出口国。
**为什么企业选择前往越南？**
企业选择前往越南，并不仅仅是因为成本考虑。越南是世界上最开放的经济体之一，吸引了大量外国直接投资。越南还有友好的关税政策，与多个国家和地区签署了双边投资协定和避免双重征税协定。对于企业来说，越南不仅可以降低成本，也可以分散风险，满足客户对供应链安全的需求。虽然越南成为了许多企业的选择，但也存在一定的风险，一些企业在越南水土不服，最终选择回流。因此，对于企业来说，选择前往越南并不是一帆风顺的。
From <[https://dongguan.jiwu.com/news/5610113.html](https://dongguan.jiwu.com/news/5610113.html)>
1. **关于制造业外迁：**
最近几年，像“苹果系”这样的大公司开始把部分生产线搬到越南和印度，主要是应对中美贸易摩擦和供应链调整的压力。比如，苹果就让一些供应商在中国以外的地方增加产能，越南和印度成了首选。但对中小企业来说，外迁就不那么现实了，毕竟像越南这样的地方，产业链配套还不够完善，基础设施也跟不上。比如，越南之前因为电力问题，有些工厂还被要求限电。这样一来，外迁的成本就很高，所以中小微企业大多还是留在中国更划算。
1. **中小企业的应对之道：**
1. **融入外资企业供应链，分担外迁成本：**对于中小企业来说，与其单打独斗，不如主动成为那些已经布局海外的大型外资企业的供应商。比如，如果某家跨国公司在越南建厂，中小企业可以提供零部件或半成品，直接服务于这家工厂。这样不仅能分担外迁的高成本，还能借助外资企业的资源和市场网络开拓新业务。尤其是像“苹果系”这样的企业，很多中小企业通过成为供应链的一环，也能在全球供应链调整中找到自己的位置。
1. **发展离岸贸易：**离岸贸易是一种不错的选择。比如，中小企业可以利用香港、新加坡等地作为离岸基地，在这些地区完成交易、结算甚至部分轻加工，然后直接出口到其他市场。这样既能规避贸易壁垒，还可以利用当地更低的税率和金融优势。同时，发展离岸贸易还能有效降低物流成本，提高资金周转效率。像一些做电子元件的公司就利用香港的离岸贸易政策，成功打开了欧美和东南亚市场。
1. **寻找区域合作机会：**中小企业也可以考虑抱团出海，联合其他企业在目标市场建立共享的生产基地或仓储中心。这样一来，不仅能降低单个企业的投资和运营成本，还能更好地共享信息和资源。比如，东莞的一些家具制造企业就以产业集群的形式进入越南，彼此分工协作，减少了开拓新市场的风险。
### **四、长期生态演变**
1. **供应链多元化**
- **产业转移**：预计未来3年，中国占亚马逊美国站商品比例从70%降至55%，东南亚份额升至25%。
- **近岸制造**：墨西哥对美出口的亚马逊商品年增长30%（2023年数据）。
From <[https://www.wearesellers.com/question/101786](https://www.wearesellers.com/question/101786)>
